# Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation (Commonwealth Marine Reserves) Proclamation 2013

made under subsections 344(1) and 350(1) of the

Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation Act 1999

**Compilation No. 1**

**Compilation date:** 11 October 2017

**Includes amendments up to:** F2017L01337

**Registered:** 24 October 2017

**About this compilation**

**This compilation**

This is a compilation of the *Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation (Commonwealth Marine Reserves) Proclamation 2013* that shows the text of the law as amended and in force on 11 October 2017 (the ***compilation date***).

The notes at the end of this compilation (the ***endnotes***) include information about amending laws and the amendment history of provisions of the compiled law.

**Uncommenced amendments**

The effect of uncommenced amendments is not shown in the text of the compiled law. Any uncommenced amendments affecting the law are accessible on the Legislation Register (www.legislation.gov.au). The details of amendments made up to, but not commenced at, the compilation date are underlined in the endnotes. For more information on any uncommenced amendments, see the series page on the Legislation Register for the compiled law.

**Application, saving and transitional provisions for provisions and amendments**

If the operation of a provision or amendment of the compiled law is affected by an application, saving or transitional provision that is not included in this compilation, details are included in the endnotes.

**Editorial changes**

For more information about any editorial changes made in this compilation, see the endnotes.

**Modifications**

If the compiled law is modified by another law, the compiled law operates as modified but the modification does not amend the text of the law. Accordingly, this compilation does not show the text of the compiled law as modified. For more information on any modifications, see the series page on the Legislation Register for the compiled law.

**Self‑repealing provisions**

If a provision of the compiled law has been repealed in accordance with a provision of the law, details are included in the endnotes.

Contents

###### 1 Name of Proclamation

This Proclamation is the *Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation (Commonwealth Marine Reserves) Proclamation 2013*.

###### 3 Authority

This Proclamation is made under subsections 344(1) and 350(1) of the *Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation Act 1999*.

###### 4 Definitions

In this Proclamation:

***Act*** means the *Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation Act 1999*.

***Fisheries Jurisdiction Line*** means the line described in annex 8 of the Torres Strait Treaty.

***Maritime boundary (French Republic) Agreement*** means the Agreement on Maritime Delimitation between the Government of Australia and the Government of the French Republic, done at Melbourne on 4 January 1982.

Note: The Agreement is in Australian Treaty Series 1983 No. 3 ([1983] ATS 3) and could in 2013 be viewed in the Australian Treaties Library on the AustLII website (http://www.austlii.edu.au).

***Maritime boundary (New Zealand) Treaty*** means the Treaty between the Government of Australia and the Government of New Zealand Establishing Certain Exclusive Economic Zone Boundaries and Continental Shelf Boundaries, done at Adelaide on 25 July 2004.

Note: The Treaty is in Australian Treaty Series 2006 No. 4 ([2006] ATS 4) and could in 2013 be viewed in the Australian Treaties Library on the AustLII website (http://www.austlii.edu.au).

***Maritime boundary (Solomon Islands) Agreement*** means the Agreement between the Government of Australia and the Government of Solomon Islands establishing Certain Sea and Seabed Boundaries, done at Honiara on 13 September 1988.

Note: The Agreement is in Australian Treaty Series 1989 No. 12 ([1989] ATS 12) and could in 2013 be viewed in the Australian Treaties Library on the AustLII website (http://www.austlii.edu.au).

***Norfolk Island*** has the same meaning as ***Territory*** in subsection 4(1) of the *Norfolk Island Act 1979*.

***Protected Zone*** means the zone established under article 10 of the Torres Strait Treaty.

Note: The Protected Zone is bounded by the line described in annex 9 of the Torres Strait Treaty.

***regulations*** means the *Environment Protection and Biodiversity Conservation Regulations 2000*.

***scheduled area*** has the same meaning as in section 7 of the *Offshore Petroleum and Greenhouse Gas Storage Act 2006*.

***State waters*** has the same meaning as ***coastal waters of the State*** in subsection 3(1) of the *Coastal Waters (State Powers) Act 1980*.

***territorial sea baseline*** means the baseline from which the breadth of the territorial sea is measured under section 7 of the *Seas and Submerged Lands Act 1973*.

***Territory waters*** has the same meaning as ***coastal waters of the Territory*** in subsection 3(1) of the *Coastal Waters (Northern Territory Powers) Act 1980*.

***Torres Strait Treaty*** means the Treaty between Australia and the Independent State of Papua New Guinea concerning Sovereignty and Maritime Boundaries in the area between the two Countries, including the area known as Torres Strait, and Related Matters, done at Sydney on 18 December 1978.

Note 1: The Treaty is in Australian Treaty Series 1985 No. 4 ([1985] ATS 4) and could in 2013 be viewed in the Australian Treaties Library on the AustLII website (http://www.austlii.edu.au).

Note 2: A number of terms used in this Proclamation are defined in the Act, including the following:

(a) Commonwealth reserve;

(b) continental shelf;

(c) exclusive economic zone;

(d) Great Barrier Reef Marine Park;

(e) IUCN category;

(f) territorial sea.

###### 5 Declaration of Commonwealth reserves

The areas described in Schedules 1 to 5 are declared to be Commonwealth reserves.

###### 6 Names of Commonwealth reserves

For paragraph 346(1)(a) of the Act, the name of each Commonwealth reserve is the name given for the reserve in Schedules 1 to 5.

###### 7 Purposes for Commonwealth reserves

For paragraph 346(1)(b) of the Act, each Commonwealth reserve mentioned in Schedules 1 to 5 has the following purposes:

(a) to protect and maintain biological diversity in the reserve;

(b) to contribute to the objectives of the national representative system of marine protected areas.

Note: The national representative system of marine protected areas (***NRSMPA***) is an Australian Government marine conservation program that ensures the conservation and sustainable use of Australia’s marine and estuarine environments. In 2013 further information about the NRSMPA was available at http://www.environment.gov.au.

###### 8 Depth of land included in Commonwealth reserves

(1) For paragraph 346(1)(c) of the Act, if a Commonwealth reserve mentioned in Schedules 1 to 5 includes land, the land is included to a depth of 1 000 metres.

(2) However, for Ashmore Reef Marine Park, the reserve includes the land to a depth of 200 metres.

###### 9 Depth of seabed included in Commonwealth reserves

(1) For paragraph 346(1)(d) of the Act, each Commonwealth reserve mentioned in Schedules 1 to 5 includes the seabed to a depth of 1 000 metres.

(2) However, for Ashmore Reef Marine Park, the reserve includes the seabed to a depth of 200 metres.

###### 10 IUCN categories of Commonwealth reserves

For paragraph 346(1)(e) of the Act, the IUCN category of each Commonwealth reserve is the IUCN category given for the reserve in Schedules 1 to 5.

###### 11 Zones of Commonwealth reserves

For subsection 346(2) of the Act, if a Commonwealth reserve mentioned in Schedules 1 to 5 is divided into zones:

(a) the zones are identified in the Schedule; and

(b) each zone is assigned an IUCN category.

###### 12 Geographic coordinates

(1) Geographic coordinates in this Proclamation are expressed in terms of the Geocentric Datum of Australia 1994.

Note: The Geocentric Datum of Australia 1994 (also known as GDA94) was published in Gazette No. GN 35 of 6 September 1995.

(2) However, the geographic coordinates in the following items in tables of this Proclamation are expressed in terms of the Australian Geodetic Datum 1966:

(a) item 1 of clause 8.3 of Schedule 3;

(b) item 1 of clause 8.5 of Schedule 3;

(c) items 1 and 2 of clause 1.3 of Schedule 4;

(d) items 1 and 2 of clause 1.5 of Schedule 4;

(e) item 38 of clause 1.6 of Schedule 4;

(f) item 1 of clause 1.13 of Schedule 4;

(g) item 2 of clause 1.13 of Schedule 4 (latitude coordinate only);

(h) item 5 of clause 1.14 of Schedule 4;

(i) item 2 of clause 1.17 of Schedule 4 (latitude coordinate only).

Note: The Australian Geodetic Datum 1966 (also known as AGD66) was published in Gazette No. 84 of 6 October 1966.

## Schedule 1—South‑west network of Marine Parks

Note: See sections 5 to 11.

### Part 1—Southern Kangaroo Island Marine Park

###### 1.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Southern Kangaroo Island Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 1.2 IUCN category

The Southern Kangaroo Island Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 1.3 Area of reserve

The Southern Kangaroo Island Marine Park consists of an area in the Southern Ocean bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

<td colspan="2">Area of reserve</td>
</tr>
<tr>

<td>Description</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1</td>
<td>The point of latitude 36°13′00″S, longitude 137°15′00.86″E</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2</td>
<td>North along the meridian of longitude 137°15′00.86″E to its intersection by the outer limit of the State waters of South Australia</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3</td>
<td>Generally south‑easterly and north‑easterly along that outer limit to its intersection by the meridian of longitude 137°43′30.36″E</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4</td>
<td>South along that meridian to its intersection by the parallel of latitude 36°13′00″S</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5</td>
<td>West along that parallel to the point of commencement</td>
</tr>
</table>

###### 2.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Western Kangaroo Island Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the marine national park zone described in clause 2.5 of this Part;

(2) The marine national park zone is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(3) The special purpose zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

###### 3.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Western Eyre Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the special purpose zone described in clause 3.5 of this Part;

(b) the marine national park zones described in clauses 3.6, 3.7 and 3.9 of this Part;

(c) the multiple use zone described in clause 3.8 of this Part.

(2) The special purpose zone described in clause 3.5 of this Part is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(3) The marine national park zones described in clauses 3.6, 3.7 and 3.9 of this Part are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(4) The multiple use zone described in clause 3.8 of this Part is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

### Part 4—Murat Marine Park

###### 4.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Murat Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 4.2 IUCN category

The Murat Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 5.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Great Australian Bight Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the special purpose zones described in clauses 5.5, 5.7 and 5.8 of this Part;

(b) the marine national park zone described in clause 5.6 of this Part;

(c) the multiple use zone described in clause 5.9 of this Part.

(2) The marine national park zone is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(3) The special purpose zones are each assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(4) The multiple use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

<td>7</td>
<td>North along that meridian to the point of commencement</td>
</tr>
</table>

### Part 8—South‑west Corner Marine Park

###### 8.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the South‑west Corner Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 8.2 IUCN category

The South‑west Corner Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 8.3 Area of reserve

The South‑west Corner Marine Park consists of an area in the Indian and Southern Oceans, excluding any State waters of Western Australia, bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

###### 8.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The South‑west Corner Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the marine national park zones described in clauses 8.5, 8.8, 8.9, 8.11, 8.13 and 8.17 of this Part;

(b) the multiple use zones described in clauses 8.6 and 8.15 of this Part;

(c) the special purpose zones (oil and gas exclusion) described in clauses 8.7 and 8.10 of this Part;

(d) the special purpose zones described in clauses 8.14 and 8.16 of this Part;

(e) the habitat protection zone described in clause 8.12 of this Part.

(2) The marine national park zones are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(3) The multiple use zones, special purpose zones (oil and gas exclusion) and special purpose zones are each assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(4) The habitat protection zone is assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

### Part 9—Bremer Marine Park

###### 9.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Bremer Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 9.2 IUCN category

The Bremer Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

### Part 10—Geographe Marine Park

###### 10.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Geographe Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 10.2 IUCN category

The Geographe Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 10.3 Area of reserve

The Geographe Marine Park consists of an area in the Indian Ocean bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

</tr>

<td>North along that meridian to the point of commencement</td>
</tr>
</table>

###### 10.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Geographe Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the special purpose zone described in clause 10.5 of this Part;

(b) the multiple use zone described in clause 10.6 of this Part;

(c) the marine national park zones described in clauses 10.7 and 10.8 of this Part.

(2) The special purpose zone and multiple use zone are each assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(3) The marine national park zones are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

### Part 13—Jurien Marine Park

###### 13.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Jurien Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 13.2 IUCN category

The Jurien Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 13.3 Area of reserve

The Jurien Marine Park consists of an area in the Indian Ocean bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

###### 13.4 Zones for reserve

(a) the special purpose zone described in clause 13.5 of this Part;

(b) the marine national park zone described in clause 13.6 of this Part.

(2) The special purpose zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(3) The marine national park zone is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(b) the multiple use zones described in clauses 14.6, 14.8 and 14.12 of this Part;

(c) the marine national park zones described in clauses 14.7, 14.10 and 14.13 of this Part;

(d) the special purpose zones described in clauses 14.9 and 14.11 of this Part.

(2) The habitat protection zone is assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

(3) The multiple use zones and special purpose zones are each assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(4) The marine national park zones are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

###### 4.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Gascoyne Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the marine national park zone described in clause 4.5;

(b) the habitat protection zones described in clauses 4.6 and 4.8;

(c) the multiple use zone described in clause 4.7.

(2) The marine national park zone is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(3) The habitat protection zones are each assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

(4) The multiple use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

###### 10.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Argo‑Rowley Terrace Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the marine national park zone described in clause 10.5;

(b) the multiple use zone described in clause 10.6.

(2) The marine national park zone is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(3) The multiple use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

### Part 11—Kimberley Marine Park

###### 11.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Kimberley Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 11.2 IUCN category

The Kimberley Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 11.3 Area of reserve

The Kimberley Marine Park consists of an area in the Indian Ocean, excluding any State waters of Western Australia, bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

<tr>
<td colspan="2">Area of reserve</td>
</tr>

<td>Description</td>
</tr>

</tr>
<tr>
<td>2</td>
<td>East along the parallel of latitude 15°00′00″S to its intersection by the meridian of longitude 123°30′00″E</td>
</tr>

</tr>
<tr>

</tr>
</table>

###### 11.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Kimberley Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the multiple use zone described in clause 11.5;

(b) the marine national park zones described in clauses 11.6 and 11.8;

(c) the habitat protection zone described in clause 11.7.

(2) The multiple use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(3) The marine national park zones are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(4) The habitat protection zone is assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

### Part 12—Ashmore Reef Marine Park

###### 12.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Ashmore Reef Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, subsection 8(2) states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and subsection 9(2) states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 12.2 IUCN category

The Ashmore Reef Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category Ia (strict nature reserve).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

### Part 13—Cartier Island Marine Park

###### 13.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Cartier Island Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 13.2 IUCN category

The Cartier Island Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category Ia (strict nature reserve).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 13.3 Area of reserve

The Cartier Island Marine Park consists of an area in the Indian Ocean enclosed within the circumference of a circle of radius 4 nautical miles, the centre of which is at the point of latitude 12°31′50.8″S, longitude 123°33′18.8″E.

## Schedule 3—North network of Marine Parks

Note: See sections 5 to 11.

## Schedule 4—Coral Sea Marine Park

Note: See sections 5 to 11.

###### 1.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Coral Sea Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 1.2 IUCN category

The Coral Sea Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 1.3 Area of reserve

The Coral Sea Marine Park consists of an area in the Coral Sea bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2">Area of reserve</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Item</td>
<td>Description</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1</td>
<td>The point of latitude 24°30′00″S, longitude 154°00′00″E</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2</td>
<td>East along the parallel of latitude 24°30′00″S to its intersection by the line described by article 1 of the Maritime boundary (French Republic) Agreement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3</td>
<td>Generally north‑westerly and north‑easterly along that line to the southernmost point on the line described by article 1 of the Maritime boundary (Solomon Islands) Agreement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4</td>
<td>Generally north‑westerly along that line to its intersection by the outer limit of the exclusive economic zone</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5</td>
<td>Generally north‑westerly along that outer limit to its intersection by the Fisheries Jurisdiction Line</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>6</td>
<td>Generally westerly and north‑westerly along that line to its intersection by the outer limit of the Protected Zone</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>7</td>
<td>South, generally south‑westerly, and west along that outer limit to its intersection by the meridian of longitude 144°00′00″E</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>8</td>
<td>South along that meridian to its intersection by the outer limit of the Great Barrier Reef Marine Park</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>9</td>
<td>Easterly, southerly and generally south‑easterly along that outer limit to the point of commencement</td>
</tr>
</table>

Note: AGD66 applies to the coordinates described in items 1 and 2 of this table.

###### 1.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Coral Sea Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the multiple use zones described in clauses 1.5, 1.13 and 1.17 of this Schedule;

(b) the marine national park zones described in clauses 1.6, 1.8 and 1.12 of this Schedule;

(c) the habitat protection zone (Coral Sea) described in clause 1.7 of this Schedule;

(d) the habitat protection zone (Seamount) described in clause 1.14 of this Schedule;

(e) the conservation park zones described in clauses 1.9, 1.10, 1.11 and 1.15 of this Schedule;

(f) the general use zone described in clause 1.16 of this Schedule.

(2) The multiple use zones are each assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(3) The marine national park zones are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(4) The habitat protection zone (Coral Sea), habitat protection zone (Seamount) and conservation park zones are each assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

(5) The general use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

## Schedule 5—Temperate East network of Marine Parks

Note: See sections 5 to 11.

### Part 1—Gifford Marine Park

###### 1.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Gifford Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 1.2 IUCN category

The Gifford Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 1.3 Area of reserve

The Gifford Marine Park consists of an area in the South Pacific Ocean bounded by the line commencing at the point described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

###### 3.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Lord Howe Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 3.2 IUCN category

The Lord Howe Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 3.3 Area of reserve

###### 3.4 Zones for reserve

(c) the recreational use zone described in clause 3.7 of this Part;

(d) the habitat protection zone (Lord Howe) described in clause 3.8 of this Part;

(e) the habitat protection zone described in clause 3.11 of this Part.

(2) The multiple use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(3) The marine national park zones are each assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

(4) The recreational use zone, habitat protection zone (Lord Howe) and habitat protection zone are each assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

###### 4.4 Zones for reserve

(1) The Central Eastern Marine Park is divided into the following zones:

(a) the habitat protection zones described in clauses 4.5 and 4.8 of this Part;

(b) the multiple use zone described in clause 4.6 of this Part;

(c) the marine national park zone described in clause 4.7 of this Part.

(2) The habitat protection zones are each assigned to IUCN category IV (habitat/species management area).

(3) The multiple use zone is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

(4) The marine national park zone is assigned to IUCN category II (national park).

### Part 5—Solitary Islands Marine Park

###### 5.1 Name of reserve

This reserve is the Solitary Islands Marine Park.

Note: Section 7 states the purposes of the reserve, section 8 states the depth of any land that may be included in the reserve and section 9 states the depth of the seabed included in the reserve.

###### 5.2 IUCN category

The Solitary Islands Marine Park is assigned to IUCN category VI (managed resource protected area).

Note: Regulation 10.03H of the regulations lists each IUCN category and Schedule 8 to the regulations sets out the Australian IUCN reserve management principles for each IUCN category.

###### 5.3 Area of reserve

The Solitary Islands Marine Park consists of an area in the South Pacific Ocean, excluding any State waters of New South Wales, bounded by the line commencing at the intersection described in item 1 of the following table and running progressively as described in the table.

## Endnotes

### Endnote 1—About the endnotes

The endnotes provide information about this compilation and the compiled law.

The following endnotes are included in every compilation:

Endnote 1—About the endnotes

Endnote 2—Abbreviation key

Endnote 3—Legislation history

Endnote 4—Amendment history

**Abbreviation key—Endnote 2**

The abbreviation key sets out abbreviations that may be used in the endnotes.

**Legislation history and amendment history—Endnotes 3 and 4**

Amending laws are annotated in the legislation history and amendment history.

The legislation history in endnote 3 provides information about each law that has amended (or will amend) the compiled law. The information includes commencement details for amending laws and details of any application, saving or transitional provisions that are not included in this compilation.

The amendment history in endnote 4 provides information about amendments at the provision (generally section or equivalent) level. It also includes information about any provision of the compiled law that has been repealed in accordance with a provision of the law.

**Editorial changes**

The *Legislation Act 2003* authorises First Parliamentary Counsel to make editorial and presentational changes to a compiled law in preparing a compilation of the law for registration. The changes must not change the effect of the law. Editorial changes take effect from the compilation registration date.

If the compilation includes editorial changes, the endnotes include a brief outline of the changes in general terms. Full details of any changes can be obtained from the Office of Parliamentary Counsel.

**Misdescribed amendments**

A misdescribed amendment is an amendment that does not accurately describe the amendment to be made. If, despite the misdescription, the amendment can be given effect as intended, the amendment is incorporated into the compiled law and the abbreviation “(md)” added to the details of the amendment included in the amendment history.

If a misdescribed amendment cannot be given effect as intended, the abbreviation “(md not incorp)” is added to the details of the amendment included in the amendment history.

### Endnote 2—Abbreviation key

| ad = added or inserted | o = order(s) |
|---|---|
| am = amended | Ord = Ordinance |
| amdt = amendment | orig = original |
| c = clause(s) | par = paragraph(s)/subparagraph(s) |
| C[x] = Compilation No. x | /sub‑subparagraph(s) |
| Ch = Chapter(s) | pres = present |
| def = definition(s) | prev = previous |
| Dict = Dictionary | (prev…) = previously |
| disallowed = disallowed by Parliament | Pt = Part(s) |
| Div = Division(s) | r = regulation(s)/rule(s) |
| ed = editorial change | reloc = relocated |
| exp = expires/expired or ceases/ceased to have | renum = renumbered |
| effect | rep = repealed |
| F = Federal Register of Legislation | rs = repealed and substituted |
| gaz = gazette | s = section(s)/subsection(s) |
| LA = *Legislation Act 2003* | Sch = Schedule(s) |
| LIA = *Legislative Instruments Act 2003* | Sdiv = Subdivision(s) |
| (md) = misdescribed amendment can be given | SLI = Select Legislative Instrument |
| effect | SR = Statutory Rules |
| (md not incorp) = misdescribed amendment | Sub‑Ch = Sub‑Chapter(s) |
| cannot be given effect | SubPt = Subpart(s) |
| mod = modified/modification | underlining = whole or part not |
| No. = Number(s) | commenced or to be commenced |

### Endnote 5—Editorial changes

In preparing this compilation for registration, the following kinds of editorial change(s) were made under the *Legislation Act 2003*.

**Kind of editorial change**

Changes to the way of referring to or expressing a measurement

**Details of editorial change**

This compilation was editorially changed to set solid all occurrences of latitude and longitude by omitting the space after the degree symbol, the space after the prime symbol and the space before the initial capital letter indicating north, south, east or west.

**Clauses 10.3 and 10.5 of Schedule 2 (table items 5 and 8, column headed “Description”)**

**Kind of editorial change**

Changes to the way of referring to or expressing a measurement

**Details of editorial change**

This compilation was editorially changed to omit all occurrences of the comma after the initial capital letter indicating south in the references to latitude and longitude in clauses 10.3 and 10.5 of Schedule 2 (table items 5 and 8, column headed “Description”), to bring these references into line with legislative drafting practice.

**Clause 10.5 of Schedule 2 (table item 8, column headed “Description”, paragraph (b))**

**Kind of editorial change**

Change to punctuation

**Details of editorial change**

Clause 10.5 of Schedule 2 (table item 8, column headed “Description”, paragraph (b)) ends in a semi‑colon despite being the last paragraph of that table item.

This compilation was editorially changed to omit the semi‑colon at the end of the paragraph to bring it into line with legislative drafting practice.

**Clause 10.5 of Schedule 2 (table item 10, column headed “Description”)**

**Kind of editorial change**

Change to the way of referring to or expressing a measurement

**Details of editorial change**

This compilation was editorially changed to omit the reference to “South” from the occurrence of latitude in clause 10.5 of Schedule 2 (table item 10, column headed “Description”) and substitute “S” to bring it into line with legislative drafting practice.
